# CLOSING PASS 5 -- `crimewidthkill` (#529): is the 141-row test complete for its contract?  Liveness token `CRIME_CLOSE5_20260914`.

VERDICT LINE FIRST: `CLOSES` (no floor gap remains in the 141-row test) or `GAP: <exact source edit to dev_sgi_ip32.c>`,
then the token.

This is a routine software-testing review of an open-source hardware emulator (GXemul) and its regression test.
"Mutant" means a deliberately modified copy of the emulator's C source used to check that the test notices the change
(standard mutation testing).  Please check the premises, not only the reasoning, and say what you could not check.
Tree: `C:\DocumentNoSnc\CC\GXEMUL\GXEMUL-SEC\` only.  File-reading seats: `src/devices/dev_sgi_ip32.c`
(`DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)`), `regress/crime_width_probe.py`, `regress/artifacts/crimewidthkill/`.

## WHY THIS PASS EXISTS, AND WHAT IT IS ASKED TO DO

The fourth closing pass found the KIND x LABEL cross missing: the pre-set and re-arm enumerations covered every branch
side, but not every accepted-access KIND (8W, 8R, 4W, 4R) at every case label; plus the HARDINT answer source (nonzero
only in a session that rowed no refused-read answer), the notice CHANNEL pinned at one cell (stepped rows cannot tell
debug() from fatal()), and a refused halfword store's bit-1 landing.  The measuring seat BUILT nine non-equivalent
131/131 survivors.  Repair 10 (141 rows) closes the cross from ONE list, `ACCEPTS` (43 kinds: {8W,8R,4W,4R} at every
label, REV's pc-window sides, CONTROL's writeflag x DOG x leftover sides, the default arm), shared by the re-arm loop and
the latch x5 prologue; adds refused lb/lh answers in the gate session; a `sh +0x42' landing; and free-running channel
sessions (one refusal each) whose notice text is read without stepping.

**So this pass is asked to VERIFY THE ENUMERATION**: is `ACCEPTS` exactly the function's accepted-access kinds, and does
every row it feeds have a channel that varies?  The chain closes on the first valid pass with zero floor breaches.

## SETTLED (recorded; re-stated because the questions depend on it)

* **The fix** (unchanged since pass 2; every seat of seven passes found it correct): a refused width is announced once per
  device instance (`int warned`, bit `CRIME_W_LEN`) and `goto crime_declined;` past the register switch to the common
  tail, which answers a read 0 and returns 1.
* **History**: 40 -> 71 -> 85 -> 111 -> 121 -> 127 -> 131 -> 141 rows; EIGHT mutation-test tables, each with a written
  PRETABLE before it ran and a mechanical prediction checker.
* **A refused read's answer** is the low `len` bytes of the `odata` the decline hands the tail (memory_writemax64).
* **Free-running vs stepped**: a stepped probe cannot tell debug() from fatal(); a free-running one can (verbose == 0).

## THE WHOLE ENCLOSING FUNCTION (the fix in place; every exit path and the common tail)

```c
DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
{
	/*
	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
	 *  physical 0x14000000.
	 *
	 *  On my real O2:
	 *
	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
	 *
	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
	 *  would work too.)
	 *
	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
	 */
	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
	size_t i;

	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);

	/*
	 *  Set crime version/revision:
	 *
	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
	 *
	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
	 */
	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;

	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	/*
	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
	 *
	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
	 *  bank #0."
	 */
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int mb_per_bank;
	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
		mb_per_bank = 32;
	else
		mb_per_bank = 128;

	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
		exit(1);
	}

	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
	size_t total_mb = 0;
	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
		else
			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;

		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
	}
	
	if (len == 8) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
		else
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
	} else if (len == 4) {
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			if (relative_addr & 4) {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
			} else {
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
			}
		} else {
			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
			if (relative_addr & 4)
				odata = (int32_t)odata;
			else
				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
		}
	} else {
		/*
		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
		 *  lhu, sb or sh anywhere in CRIME did it (witnessed at rung 3
		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.
		 *
		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
		 */
		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
		}
		goto crime_declined;
	}

	switch (relative_addr) {

	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
		/*
		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
		 *
		 *  The address where this happens is:
		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
		 *
		 *  4.18 works too.
		 *
		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
		 */
		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
			if (cpu->is_32bit)
				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
			else
				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
			/*
			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
			 */
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
			}
			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
				int j;

				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
				    "reboot" command:  */
				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;

				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
			}

			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;

			if (idata != 0)
				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
		}
		break;

	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
		break;

	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
		// No warning.
		break;

	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
		crime_update_crime_time(d);
		break;

	default:
		if (writeflag==MEM_READ) {
			debug("[ crime: read from 0x%x, len=%i:",
			    (int)relative_addr, len);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" ]\n");
		} else {
			debug("[ crime: write to 0x%x:", (int)relative_addr);
			for (i=0; i<len; i++)
				debug(" %02x", data[i]);
			debug(" (len=%i) ]\n", len);
		}
	}

 crime_declined:
	/*  #529: a declined access jumps here, skipping the register switch,
	    and is still answered.  */
	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = preserved_CRIME_HARDINT;
	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);

	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);

	return 1;
}
```

```
     CRIME_CONTROL by its exact label 0x08 and reads the RAW idata, so a 32-bit write to the high half, or a halfword at
     +0x08, carries bit 11 into HARD_RESET, while NetBSD's +0x0c write does nothing.  A round that fixes that lane
     removes or moves these channels and owes their re-derivation -- the sw08 controls will go red to say so. ***

     found members (F2: `|= len', `|= relative_addr', `<< writeflag'; closing pass: keyed on CRIME_CONTROL, on

     closing pass: the ADDRESSED lanes passed the 0x10000001 seed 121/121 on the 121-row test; glm, third closing pass:
     the 4-byte path's half, `reg >> 32', passed the 0x0101000010000001 one).  [A selection CONFINED above byte 1 --
     `& 0xffff0000' -- answers 0 to every lb/lh by construction and is EQUIVALENT; this said "ANY selection ... shows"
     until the kimi CLI seat, fourth closing pass.]  And the answer SOURCE other than the addressed register: every
     lvalue the function reads is nonzero where a refused answer is read, including HARDINT -- latched only in the gate
     session, where refused lb/lh now read it (kimi-k3:cloud, the Opus seat, fourth closing pass: a decline answering
     `d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / 8]' scored 131/131 on the 131-row test)                                          gate lb v0, gate lh v0.
     CONTROL is answered at both widths (ctl lb v0, ctl v0; the Opus
     third-closing seat, measured: `odata = 1' keyed on len 1 at CONTROL passed the lh-only row); its low byte is 0, so
     only a CONSTANT answer can show at lb there.  TIME: at the time rows' refused reads TIME is a multiple of 66 and is
     0 in 42 of 800 logged cells (the same seat -- this said "0.3% of runs, 66-990 includes 256, 512, 768", and those
     are not multiples of 66), so the register cell is pinned by a SEEDED odd TIME instead     time sd lb v0, time sd lh v0
     and in the far block and the middle block over a NONZERO slot, at both widths (Opus closing: a refused read
     >= 0x200 answering the register; Opus, second closing pass: the 2-byte cell there)
                                                                   top rd v0, top rd2 v0, mid lb v0, mid lh v0
  10. the decline HOLDS WHILE AN INTERRUPT IS LATCHED: HARDINT set by the guest through MACE and the CRIME line
      asserted, a refused sh 0x0800 at CONTROL still does not stop the CPUs (the Opus closing seat: `if
      (!d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / 8])' -- a field the tail reads -- and `if (!d->prev_asserted)' passed the 85-row
      version)                                        gate run, with gate hardint and gate line as its controls
      and a refused READ answers 0 while HARDINT is latched -- the answer SOURCE item 9 names (fourth closing pass)
                                                                                                 gate lb v0, gate lh v0

DISCLOSED, not rowed:
  * `goto crime_declined' -> `return 1' is EQUIVALENT on every channel: memory_rw pre-zeroes a device read buffer
    (#95, src/cpus/memory_rw.c -- `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)' then `memset(data, 0, len);', immediately above
    `paddr -= mem->devices[i].baseaddr;'), and on a declined access nothing between the entry and the tail writes
    HARDINT or SOFTINT, so the tail's restore and recompute are identities.  Measured 40/40 (the then 40-row test) in kill table 1.
  * a fall-through confined to the two label cells whose arms have NO body for that access, both EQUIVALENT:
      CRIME_DOG (0x30)       `No warning.' -- the arm is `break;'
      CRIME_CONTROL, reads   the arm acts only on MEM_WRITE
    Each measured as a survivor in kill table 2 (`!= CRIME_DOG', the CONTROL-read-only fall-through).
  * ACCEPT cells at unrowed slots: the rows touch accepted accesses at +0x00, +0x04, +0x08, +0x10, +0x18, +0x30,
    +0x34, +0x38, +0x210, +0x214, +0x1f8 and +0x278 (not an exhaustive list of what the window accepts); an accept arm
    narrowed by an address strictly INSIDE the window at an unrowed slot (e.g. `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0x100',
    kill table 2's acc8mid, 71/71 on the 71-row test) is not seen.
  * an effect ADDED to the decline arm and keyed on ONE UNNAMED offset (item 9 rows the READ answer at every named
    label) -- a refused store that lands only at
    +0x31, a declined read answering nonzero only at +0x20, a silent decline only at +0x100, a fall-through only at
    +0x40 (kimi CLI, glm, F2).  The rows pin the refusal floor at every observable LABEL and at eight default-arm
    offsets; the keeps rows catch a landing at CRIME_DOG, INTMASK, the far block and the middle block.  An effect
    keyed on a single ADDRESS BIT is NOT in this class: every bit 0-9 is carried both ways by a refused access of each
    kind that can carry it (the mid session sets bits 0-8, the far block bit 9; an aligned 2-byte access never sets
    bit 0; kimi-k3:cloud, second closing pass, found bits 7 and 8 carried by no refusal at all; codex and the Opus seat,
    fourth closing pass, found 2W LANDING at bit 1 set carried by no read-back store -- now +0x42, `lo keeps').  Note
    `relative_addr / 8': a refused halfword at +0x1e would land on INTMASK's slot, not at +0x1e; the 2W session's
    +0x1e store is not read back, and the INTMASK landing is carried by `imask keeps'.  A landing at
    CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20, not a switch label) is in this class (deepseek, third closing pass).  ONE NAMED CELL IS
    OPEN AND SAID SO (kimi-k3:cloud, closing pass): a refused sb/sh that LANDS only at CRIME_TIME stores <= 0xffff,
    which the time rows' 0.1 s threshold cannot tell from the 66-990 the first tick adds -- clause (e) debt.  At INTSTAT
    (the tail recomputes it), CONTROL and REV (the prelude rewrites them) such a landing is EQUIVALENT.
  * an accepted WRITE (4 or 8 bytes) at +0x00 or +0x08 shrunk away: the prelude rewrites CRIME_REV and CRIME_CONTROL on
    every access, so no round trip there can observe whether the store was accepted (the CONTROL arm's effects are
    rowed separately as sw08 halts / sw08 msg ctl).  The 4R-high cell at +0x00 is carried by `rev pc ctl' (its redirect
    needs the access ACCEPTED), not by `devsig hi', whose 0 a refused read also returns.
  * a decline answering a selection of the register's bytes at CRIME_REV, CRIME_CONTROL or CRIME_TIME that the model
    holds at 0 there -- the ADDRESSED lanes (bytes 0-1 at a label), or the 4-byte half above the low word -- is
    EQUIVALENT (the prelude rewrites REV = 0x11 and CONTROL = 0x100 on every access; TIME is kept to 32 bits).  At
    INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG every byte is seeded nonzero, so any selection shows (Opus, second closing pass; glm, third).
  * a latch keyed on a JOINT predicate (e.g. only 2-byte refusals at CRIME_TIME), or on one unnamed offset other than the
    default-arm cells the latch rows refuse at: clause (e), the single-axis generating set being the floor.
  * a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch of the CONTROL arm: an accepted write that takes it stops every
    CPU, so nothing can follow it in the session.  Unrowable, not merely unrowed.
  * a fall-through only at CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20), which the switch does not name -- measured 71/71 (the 71-row test) by the Opus seat
    (F2, `sft'); it is the single-unnamed-offset class of the bullet above.
  * a FUNCTION-STATIC latch in place of `d->warned' is NOT equivalent in general -- two IP32 machines in one gxemul
    process would share it -- and this single-machine probe cannot see it (kimi-k3:cloud, F2; the pass-2 Opus seat had
    recorded it "equivalent", which holds only for one machine per process).
  * widths 3, 5, 6, 7 (no MIPS load/store issues them); the unaligned-pair instructions (lwl/lwr/swl/swr, ldl/ldr) are
    not rowed.  The RAM-size checks ABOVE the width arm (`-M 48' exits at the first CRIME access) are filed, not here.
  * independence between two CRIME instances (the latch is per instance by construction; IP32 has one CRIME).

```

## THE MUTANTS OF TABLE 8, verbatim (repair-10 samples, then the Opus close-4 seat's nine)

```python
M = {
    "base": [],
    # claim 1 (deepseek): pre-set/re-arm keyed on an accepted CONTROL write of exactly 0x0200, inserted AFTER the
    # DOG_ENABLE clear block (where the seat believed idata==0x200 is a non-rowed accepted cell).
    "c1_deepseek": [(DOGBLK, DOGBLK + "\t\t\tif (idata == CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE)\n\t\t\t\td->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n")],
    # claim 2 (kimi-k3): a declined read answers HARDINT instead of 0.
    "c2_kimik3": [(GOTO, "\t\todata = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];\n" + GOTO)],
    # claim 3 (codex): the announce channel becomes debug() at offsets with bit 3 set, fatal() elsewhere.
    "c3_codex_dbg": [(FATAL, "\t\t\t(relative_addr & 8 ? debug : fatal)(\"[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined.\"\n")],
    # claim 4 (kimi CLI): a re-arm keyed on an ACCEPTED WRITE reaching the INTSTAT/INTMASK arm.
    "c4_kimicli": [(INTARM,
                    "\tcase CRIME_INTSTAT:\t/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */\n"

                    "\t\tif (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)\n\t\t\td->warned = 0;\n"

                    "\t\tbreak;\n")],
    # claim 5 (codex): a refused 2-byte store LANDS at offsets with address bit 1 set.

                       "\tcase CRIME_INTMASK:\t/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */\n"
                       "\t\tif (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && relative_addr == CRIME_INTSTAT)\n\t\t\td->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;\n"
                       "\t\tcrime_interrupt_reassert(d);\n"
                       "\t\tbreak;\n")],
    "d6_chan_len2": [(FATAL, "\t\t\t(len == 2 ? debug : fatal)(\"[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined.\"\n")],
    "d7_rearm_wrev": [(REVCASE, REVCASE + "\t\tif (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)\n\t\t\td->warned = 0;\n")],
}

```

## QUESTIONS

1. **Verify the enumeration.**  Is `ACCEPTS` exactly the set of accepted-access kinds `DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)` distinguishes
   (every width it accepts, at every case label and every side of every `if` an accepted access takes)?  Name any kind it
   omits, any it lists that the function cannot take, and any row it feeds whose channel cannot vary under the mutant the
   row claims.
2. **Is there a floor gap in the 141-row test?**  The smallest source edit to `dev_sgi_ip32.c` that undoes part of the
   fix's behaviour and still scores 141/141, as an exact edit, with the row you expected to catch it and why it cannot.
   Items on the header's DISCLOSED list are outside the question; say if one is disclosed wrongly.
3. **Which statement in this brief, in POSTTABLE 8, or in the probe header, is wrong?**


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

272:## THE TEST'S HEADER -- CONTRACT and DISCLOSED, verbatim
273:
274:```
275:CONTRACT -- the edits this detector is designed to catch (clause (e)), each MEASURED in a kill table under
276:artifacts/crimewidthkill/ (killtable_20260913/ through killtable8_20260914/):
277:  1. the kill reinstated verbatim, or any other host death at the width arm, before or after the announce
278:                                                       <op> surv (six widths), hi surv, latch surv, sb08 run
279:  2. FAULT: the width arm returning 0 (a guest data bus error, EXCEPTION_DBE)   lb/lh/sb/sh pc and exc, lb/lh/lbu/lhu v0
280:  3. the refused access FALLING INTO THE REGISTER SWITCH -- the goto lost, or narrowed by width, direction, or ADDRESS.
281:     The switch's observable arms, per refusal cell:
282:        CRIME_CONTROL (+0x08), writes     sb 0x01 prints the unimplemented-control fatal    sb08 msg
283:                                          sh 0x0800 stops every CPU (HARD_RESET)            sh08 run, sh08 pc
284:                                          and the arm's three TESTED idata bits (DOG 0x200, RESET 0x800,
285:                                          ENDIANESS 0x100) each both ways at 2W: sh 0x0201,
286:                                          0x0900, 0x0a00 with sh08 0x0800       sh08b msg, sh08b run
287:                                          plus ALL-ONES refused writes, sb 0xff and sh 0xffff (kimi CLI,
288:                                          closing pass), so a fall-through keyed on any bit a 1- or 2-byte
289:                                          store can carry shows (bits above 15 never reach a refused access,
290:                                          so a conjunct on them is equivalent)  sb08 msg, sh08b msg/run
291:        and the refused stores at INTSTAT, INTMASK and TIME carry all-ones (0xff / 0xffff) for the same reason
292:        CRIME_REV (+0x00), reads AND writes, from pc 0xbfc05180: the PROM-probe hack sets pc = ra (it does not test
293:                                          the direction)                                    lb/lh/sb/sh rev pc
294:        CRIME_INTMASK (+0x18), CRIME_INTSTAT (+0x10), all four cells: the arm calls crime_interrupt_reassert(),
295:                                          which raises cause.IP2 after a SOFTINT store   imask/istat lb, sb, lh, sh
296:        CRIME_TIME (+0x38), all four cells: the arm advances TIME, which the NEXT read shows   time lb, sb, lh, sh
297:        the default arm (any unlabelled offset): a debug() line, printed while stepping      lb/lh/sb/sh nodflt,
298:                                          at +0x07, +0x06, +0x1f, +0x1e, +0x27f  hi nodflt, and
299:                                          +0x1ff, +0x1fe, +0x1fc                 mid nodflt
300:     Each channel carries a LIVE control on an accepted width that shows it can vary:
301:        rev pc ctl (lw redirects), sw08 halts (sw 0x800 stops the CPUs), sw08 msg ctl (sw 0x01 prints the text),
302:        imask/istat pre + ctl (IP2 low after the setup; an accepted lw sets it), time lb/sb/lh/sh ctl (an accepted ld
303:        moves TIME), dflt ctl (lw +0x04 prints the default-arm line).
304:     The INTSTAT/INTMASK/TIME rows came from the pass-2 Opus seat, which MEASURED a decline narrowed to skip those
305:     labels passing the 40-row version of this detector 40/40 (opus_pass2_20260913/OPUS_REPORT.md); their 2-byte cells
306:     from the F2 pass (grok, glm): `len != 2 || relative_addr != CRIME_INTSTAT' passed the 71-row version.  So every
307:     observable label carries the whole {1,2} x {R,W} refusal floor (CONTROL's reads and DOG have no body: below).
308:     *** THE sh08 AND sw08 CHANNELS EXIST BECAUSE OF A SEPARATE DEFECT, `crimectllane' (filed): the switch matches
309:     CRIME_CONTROL by its exact label 0x08 and reads the RAW idata, so a 32-bit write to the high half, or a halfword at
310:     +0x08, carries bit 11 into HARD_RESET, while NetBSD's +0x0c write does nothing.  A round that fixes that lane
311:     removes or moves these channels and owes their re-derivation -- the sw08 controls will go red to say so. ***

12:## WHY THIS PASS EXISTS, AND WHAT IT IS ASKED TO DO

14:The fourth closing pass found the KIND x LABEL cross missing: the pre-set and re-arm enumerations covered every branch
15:side, but not every accepted-access KIND (8W, 8R, 4W, 4R) at every case label; plus the HARDINT answer source (nonzero

17:debug() from fatal()), and a refused halfword store's bit-1 landing.  The measuring seat BUILT nine non-equivalent
18:131/131 survivors.  Repair 10 (141 rows) closes the cross from ONE list, `ACCEPTS` (43 kinds: {8W,8R,4W,4R} at every
19:label, REV's pc-window sides, CONTROL's writeflag x DOG x leftover sides, the default arm), shared by the re-arm loop and
20:the latch x5 prologue; adds refused lb/lh answers in the gate session; a `sh +0x42' landing; and free-running channel
21:sessions (one refusal each) whose notice text is read without stepping.
22:
23:**So this pass is asked to VERIFY THE ENUMERATION**: is `ACCEPTS` exactly the function's accepted-access kinds, and does
24:every row it feeds have a channel that varies?  The chain closes on the first valid pass with zero floor breaches.

26:## SETTLED (recorded; re-stated because the questions depend on it)
27:
28:* **The fix** (unchanged since pass 2; every seat of seven passes found it correct): a refused width is announced once per
29:  device instance (`int warned`, bit `CRIME_W_LEN`) and `goto crime_declined;` past the register switch to the common
30:  tail, which answers a read 0 and returns 1.
31:* **History**: 40 -> 71 -> 85 -> 111 -> 121 -> 127 -> 131 -> 141 rows; EIGHT mutation-test tables, each with a written
32:  PRETABLE before it ran and a mechanical prediction checker.
33:* **A refused read's answer** is the low `len` bytes of the `odata` the decline hands the tail (memory_writemax64).
34:* **Free-running vs stepped**: a stepped probe cannot tell debug() from fatal(); a free-running one can (verbose == 0).

36:## THE WHOLE ENCLOSING FUNCTION (the fix in place; every exit path and the common tail)
37:
38:```c
39:DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
40:{
41:	/*
42:	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
43:	 *  physical 0x14000000.
44:	 *
45:	 *  On my real O2:
46:	 *
47:	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
48:	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
49:	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
50:	 *
51:	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
52:	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
53:	 *  would work too.)
54:	 *
55:	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
56:	 */
57:	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
58:	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
59:	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
60:	size_t i;
61:
62:	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
63:		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
64:
65:	/*
66:	 *  Set crime version/revision:
67:	 *
68:	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
69:	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
70:	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
71:	 *
72:	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
73:	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
74:	 */
75:	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;
76:
77:	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
78:
79:	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
80:		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
81:		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
82:
83:	/*
84:	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
85:	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
86:	 *
87:	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
88:	 *  bank #0."
89:	 */
90:	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
91:		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
92:		exit(1);
93:	}
94:
95:	int mb_per_bank;
96:	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
97:		mb_per_bank = 32;
98:	else
99:		mb_per_bank = 128;
100:
101:	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
102:		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
103:			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
104:			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
105:		exit(1);
106:	}
107:
108:	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
109:	size_t total_mb = 0;
110:	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
111:		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
112:		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
113:			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
114:				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
115:		else
116:			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;
117:
118:		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
119:	}
120:	
121:	if (len == 8) {
122:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
123:			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
124:		else
125:			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
126:	} else if (len == 4) {
127:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
128:			if (relative_addr & 4) {
129:				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
130:				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
131:			} else {
132:				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
133:				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
134:			}
135:		} else {
136:			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
137:			if (relative_addr & 4)
138:				odata = (int32_t)odata;
139:			else
140:				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
141:		}
142:	} else {
143:		/*
144:		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
145:		 *  lhu, sb or sh anywhere in CRIME did it (witnessed at rung 3
146:		 *  on an unmodified -E sgi -e ip32, 6/6).  The width is now
147:		 *  DECLINED and announced once, the #498 sgi_de shape: a read
148:		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.
149:		 *
150:		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
151:		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
152:		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
153:		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
154:		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
155:		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
156:		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
157:		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
158:		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
159:		 */
160:		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
161:			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
162:			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
163:			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
164:		}
165:		goto crime_declined;
166:	}
167:
168:	switch (relative_addr) {
169:
170:	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
171:		/*
172:		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
173:		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
174:		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
175:		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
176:		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
177:		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
178:		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
179:		 *
180:		 *  The address where this happens is:
181:		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
182:		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
183:		 *
184:		 *  4.18 works too.
185:		 *
186:		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
187:		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
188:		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
189:		 */
190:		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
191:			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
192:			if (cpu->is_32bit)
193:				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
194:			else
195:				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
196:		}
197:		break;
198:
199:	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
200:		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
201:		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
202:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
203:			/*
204:			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
205:			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
206:			 */
207:			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
208:				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
209:			}
210:			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
211:				int j;
212:
213:				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
214:				    "reboot" command:  */
215:				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
216:					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;
217:
218:				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
219:			}
220:
221:			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
222:
223:			if (idata != 0)
224:				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
225:				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
226:		}
227:		break;
228:
229:	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
230:	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */
231:		crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
232:		break;
233:
234:	case CRIME_DOG:		/*  0x030  */
235:		// No warning.
236:		break;
237:
238:	case CRIME_TIME:	/*  0x038  */
239:		crime_update_crime_time(d);
240:		break;

419:    Each measured as a survivor in kill table 2 (`!= CRIME_DOG', the CONTROL-read-only fall-through).
420:  * ACCEPT cells at unrowed slots: the rows touch accepted accesses at +0x00, +0x04, +0x08, +0x10, +0x18, +0x30,
421:    +0x34, +0x38, +0x210, +0x214, +0x1f8 and +0x278 (not an exhaustive list of what the window accepts); an accept arm
422:    narrowed by an address strictly INSIDE the window at an unrowed slot (e.g. `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0x100',
423:    kill table 2's acc8mid, 71/71 on the 71-row test) is not seen.
424:  * an effect ADDED to the decline arm and keyed on ONE UNNAMED offset (item 9 rows the READ answer at every named
425:    label) -- a refused store that lands only at
426:    +0x31, a declined read answering nonzero only at +0x20, a silent decline only at +0x100, a fall-through only at
427:    +0x40 (kimi CLI, glm, F2).  The rows pin the refusal floor at every observable LABEL and at eight default-arm
428:    offsets; the keeps rows catch a landing at CRIME_DOG, INTMASK, the far block and the middle block.  An effect
429:    keyed on a single ADDRESS BIT is NOT in this class: every bit 0-9 is carried both ways by a refused access of each
430:    kind that can carry it (the mid session sets bits 0-8, the far block bit 9; an aligned 2-byte access never sets
431:    bit 0; kimi-k3:cloud, second closing pass, found bits 7 and 8 carried by no refusal at all; codex and the Opus seat,
432:    fourth closing pass, found 2W LANDING at bit 1 set carried by no read-back store -- now +0x42, `lo keeps').  Note
433:    `relative_addr / 8': a refused halfword at +0x1e would land on INTMASK's slot, not at +0x1e; the 2W session's
434:    +0x1e store is not read back, and the INTMASK landing is carried by `imask keeps'.  A landing at
435:    CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20, not a switch label) is in this class (deepseek, third closing pass).  ONE NAMED CELL IS
436:    OPEN AND SAID SO (kimi-k3:cloud, closing pass): a refused sb/sh that LANDS only at CRIME_TIME stores <= 0xffff,
437:    which the time rows' 0.1 s threshold cannot tell from the 66-990 the first tick adds -- clause (e) debt.  At INTSTAT
438:    (the tail recomputes it), CONTROL and REV (the prelude rewrites them) such a landing is EQUIVALENT.
439:  * an accepted WRITE (4 or 8 bytes) at +0x00 or +0x08 shrunk away: the prelude rewrites CRIME_REV and CRIME_CONTROL on
440:    every access, so no round trip there can observe whether the store was accepted (the CONTROL arm's effects are
441:    rowed separately as sw08 halts / sw08 msg ctl).  The 4R-high cell at +0x00 is carried by `rev pc ctl' (its redirect
442:    needs the access ACCEPTED), not by `devsig hi', whose 0 a refused read also returns.
443:  * a decline answering a selection of the register's bytes at CRIME_REV, CRIME_CONTROL or CRIME_TIME that the model
444:    holds at 0 there -- the ADDRESSED lanes (bytes 0-1 at a label), or the 4-byte half above the low word -- is
445:    EQUIVALENT (the prelude rewrites REV = 0x11 and CONTROL = 0x100 on every access; TIME is kept to 32 bits).  At
446:    INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG every byte is seeded nonzero, so any selection shows (Opus, second closing pass; glm, third).
447:  * a latch keyed on a JOINT predicate (e.g. only 2-byte refusals at CRIME_TIME), or on one unnamed offset other than the
448:    default-arm cells the latch rows refuse at: clause (e), the single-axis generating set being the floor.
449:  * a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch of the CONTROL arm: an accepted write that takes it stops every
450:    CPU, so nothing can follow it in the session.  Unrowable, not merely unrowed.
451:  * a fall-through only at CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20), which the switch does not name -- measured 71/71 (the 71-row test) by the Opus seat
452:    (F2, `sft'); it is the single-unnamed-offset class of the bullet above.
453:  * a FUNCTION-STATIC latch in place of `d->warned' is NOT equivalent in general -- two IP32 machines in one gxemul
454:    process would share it -- and this single-machine probe cannot see it (kimi-k3:cloud, F2; the pass-2 Opus seat had
455:    recorded it "equivalent", which holds only for one machine per process).
456:  * widths 3, 5, 6, 7 (no MIPS load/store issues them); the unaligned-pair instructions (lwl/lwr/swl/swr, ldl/ldr) are
457:    not rowed.  The RAM-size checks ABOVE the width arm (`-M 48' exits at the first CRIME access) are filed, not here.
458:  * independence between two CRIME instances (the latch is per instance by construction; IP32 has one CRIME).
459:

661:+#  +0x1f8 (bits 3-8 set), 1W at +0x27f (bit 9, a write), 2W at CONTROL; and one per remaining label.
662:+def free_one(op, off, val):
663:+    A = Addr(CODE)
664:+    here = A.take(2)
665:+    if op[0] == "l":
666:+        setup, ins = ["at=0x%x" % (CRIME + off), "v0=0x%x" % SENT], enc(op, "at", "v0")
667:+    else:
668:+        setup, ins = ["a3=0x%x" % (CRIME + off), "t0=0x%x" % val], enc(op, "a3", "t0")
669:+    c = setup + ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (here, ins), "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (here + 4, OP_NOP),
670:+                 "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (here + 8, OP_B_SELF), "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (here + 12, OP_NOP),
671:+                 "pc=0x%x" % here, "<RUN>"]
672:+    t, clean = session(c)
673:+    ran = t.split("continue", 1)[1] if "continue" in t else ""
674:+    ln = 1 if op in ("lb", "lbu", "sb") else 2
675:+    return ran.count("unimplemented len=%d at 0x%x -- declined.  (once) ]" % (ln, off))
676:+
677:+
678:+for op, off, val, nm in (("lh", 0x1f8, 0, "free 2r said"), ("sb", 0x27f, 0xff, "free 1w said"),
679:+                         ("sh", 0x08, 0, "free 2w said"), ("lb", 0x00, 0, "free rev said"),
680:+                         ("lb", 0x10, 0, "free istat said"), ("lb", 0x18, 0, "free imask said"),
681:+                         ("lb", 0x30, 0, "free dog said"), ("lb", 0x38, 0, "free time said")):
682:+    row(nm, free_one(op, off, val), 1, "free-running %s +0x%x: the fatal() announce printed" % (op, off))

98:void crime_interrupt_reassert(struct crime_data *d)
99:{
100:	uint64_t status =
101:		d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
102:		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
103:
104:	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] = status;
105:
106:	status &= d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / sizeof(uint64_t)];
107:
108:#if 0
109:	printf("CRIME SOFTINT=0x%08x HARDINT=0x%08x => 0x%08x, INTMASK=0x%08x\n",
110:		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)],
111:		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)],
112:		(uint32_t)status,
113:		(uint32_t)d->reg[CRIME_INTMASK / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
114:#endif
115:	
116:	int asserted = !!status;
117:
118:	if (asserted && !d->prev_asserted)
119:		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
120:	else if (!asserted && d->prev_asserted)
121:		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
122:	
123:	d->prev_asserted = asserted;
124:}
125:
126:/*
127: *  crime_interrupt_assert():
128: *  crime_interrupt_deassert():
129: */
130:void crime_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
131:{
132:	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) interrupt->extra;
133:	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |= interrupt->line;
134:	//printf("CRIME asserting 0x%08x\n", interrupt->line);
135:	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
136:}
137:void crime_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
138:{
139:	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) interrupt->extra;
140:	d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] &= ~interrupt->line;
141:	//printf("CRIME deasserting 0x%08x\n", interrupt->line);
142:	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
143:}
144:
145:
146:void crime_update_crime_time(struct crime_data* d)
147:{
148:	struct timeval tv;
149:	
150:	gettimeofday(&tv, NULL);
151:
152:	uint64_t microseconds = tv.tv_sec * 1000000 + tv.tv_usec;
153:	if (d->last_microseconds == 0)
154:		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
155:
156:	int64_t delta = microseconds - d->last_microseconds;
157:
158:	if (delta < 0) {
159:		fatal("[ crime_update_crime_time: host system time went backwards? ]\n");
160:		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
161:		delta = 0;
162:	}
163:
164:	// The delta to add is 66 per microsecond.
165:	int64_t to_add = delta * 66;
166:
167:	if (to_add >= 1) {
168:		// NetBSD says CRIME_TIME_MASK = 0x0000ffffffffffffULL
169:		// but my O2 seems to use only the lower 32 bits as an
170:		// _unsigned_ value. (TODO: Double-check this again.)
171:		d->reg[CRIME_TIME / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
172:			(uint32_t)(d->reg[CRIME_TIME / sizeof(uint64_t)] + to_add);
173:
174:		d->last_microseconds = microseconds;
175:	}
176:}
177:

179:/*
180: *  dev_crime_tick():
181: *
182: *  Updates CRIME_TIME (at 66 MHz) and reassert CRIME interrupts.
183: */
184:DEVICE_TICK(crime)
185:{
186:	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
187:
188:	crime_update_crime_time(d);
189:	crime_interrupt_reassert(d);
190:}

193:DEVICE_ACCESS(crime)
194:{
195:	/*
196:	 *  The CRIME is memory mapped as 0x80 bytes, starting at
197:	 *  physical 0x14000000.
198:	 *
199:	 *  On my real O2:
200:	 *
201:	 *  0x14000080..0x140000ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
202:	 *  0x14000100..0x1400017f is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
203:	 *  0x14000180..0x140001ff is the same as 0x14000000..0x1400007f.
204:	 *
205:	 *  (I assume that real code will never access the above "mirrors",
206:	 *  but it would be trivial to add redirection so that such code
207:	 *  would work too.)
208:	 *
209:	 *  Memory control then starts at 0x14000200.
210:	 */
211:	struct crime_data *d = (struct crime_data *) extra;
212:	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
213:	uint64_t preserved_CRIME_HARDINT = d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)];
214:	size_t i;
215:
216:	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
217:		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
218:
219:	/*
220:	 *  Set crime version/revision:
221:	 *
222:	 *  This might not be the most elegant or correct solution, but it
223:	 *  seems that the IP32 PROM likes 0x11 for machines without graphics,
224:	 *  and 0xa1 for machines with graphics.
225:	 *
226:	 *  NetBSD 2.0 complains about "unknown" crime for 0x11, but I guess
227:	 *  that's something one has to live with.  (TODO?)
228:	 */
229:	d->reg[CRIME_REV / sizeof(uint64_t)] = d->use_fb? 0xa1 : 0x11;
230:
231:	d->reg[CRIME_CONTROL / sizeof(uint64_t)] = CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
232:
233:	d->reg[CRIME_INTSTAT / sizeof(uint64_t)] =
234:		(d->reg[CRIME_SOFTINT / sizeof(uint64_t)] |
235:		d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / sizeof(uint64_t)]);
236:
237:	/*
238:	 *  Amount of memory.  Bit 8 of bank control set ==> 128MB instead
239:	 *  of 32MB per bank (?)
240:	 *
241:	 *  According to OpenBSD, "Empty banks are reported as duplicates of
242:	 *  bank #0."
243:	 */
244:	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb > 1024) {
245:		fatal("[ sgi_crime: SGI O2 can not have more than 1024 MB RAM ]\n");
246:		exit(1);
247:	}
248:
249:	int mb_per_bank;
250:	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb <= 256)
251:		mb_per_bank = 32;
252:	else
253:		mb_per_bank = 128;
254:
255:	if (cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb % mb_per_bank) {
256:		fatal("[ sgi_crime: for up to 256 MB RAM, RAM size needs to be divisible "
257:			"by 32 MB. for larger RAM sizes (up to 1024 MB), it needs to be "
258:			"divisible by 128 MB. ]\n");
259:		exit(1);
260:	}
261:
262:	int flag_for_128MB = mb_per_bank == 128 ? 0x100 : 0x000;
263:	size_t total_mb = 0;
264:	for (int bank = 0; bank < 8; ++bank) {
265:		int b = mb_per_bank == 128 ? (bank << 2) : bank;
266:		if (total_mb >= cpu->machine->physical_ram_in_mb)
267:			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] =
268:				d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + 0];
269:		else
270:			d->reg[CRIME_MEM_BANK_CTRL0 / sizeof(uint64_t) + bank] = flag_for_128MB | b;
271:
272:		total_mb += mb_per_bank;
273:	}
274:	
275:	if (len == 8) {
276:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
277:			d->reg[relative_addr / 8] = idata;
278:		else
279:			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
280:	} else if (len == 4) {
281:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
282:			if (relative_addr & 4) {
283:				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= ~0xffffffffULL;
284:				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint32_t)idata;
285:			} else {
286:				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] &= 0xffffffffULL;
287:				d->reg[relative_addr / 8] |= (uint64_t)(idata << 32ULL);
288:			}
289:		} else {
290:			odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8];
291:			if (relative_addr & 4)
292:				odata = (int32_t)odata;
293:			else
294:				odata = (int32_t)(odata >> 32);
295:		}
296:	} else {
297:		/*
298:		 *  #529: THIS USED TO END THE PROCESS.  One guest lb, lbu, lh,
302:		 *  is answered 0 through the common tail, a write is dropped.303:		 *
304:		 *  The goto skips the register switch below, and that is the
305:		 *  point of it: falling through would let a halfword 0x0800
306:		 *  written at CRIME_CONTROL stop every CPU, a narrow access to
307:		 *  CRIME_REV from the PROM probe loop redirect the PC, one at
308:		 *  INTSTAT or INTMASK reassert the interrupt line, and one at
309:		 *  CRIME_TIME advance the clock -- all measured.  It is not
310:		 *  `return 0' either: that raises a guest bus error
311:		 *  (EXCEPTION_DBE, cpus/memory_rw.c), and what real CRIME does
312:		 *  on a narrow cycle is not documented here.
313:		 */
314:		if (!(d->warned & CRIME_W_LEN)) {
315:			d->warned |= CRIME_W_LEN;
316:			fatal("[ crime: unimplemented len=%i at 0x%x -- declined."
317:			    "  (once) ]\n", (int) len, (int) relative_addr);
318:		}
319:		goto crime_declined;
320:	}
321:
322:	switch (relative_addr) {
323:
324:	case CRIME_REV:		/*  0x000  */
325:		/*
326:		 *  A contender for winning a prize for the worst hack
327:		 *  in history:  the IP32 PROM probes the CPU caches during
328:		 *  bootup, but they are not really emulated, so it fails.
329:		 *  During the probe, the CRIME_REV is read a lot. By
330:		 *  "returning" from the probe function, i.e. jumping to ra,
331:		 *  when this register is read the second time, the probe
332:		 *  can be skipped, and the PROM thus runs further.
333:		 *
334:		 *  The address where this happens is:
335:		 *	0xbfc0517c	PROM v2.3
336:		 *	0xbfc051ac	PROM v4.13
337:		 *
338:		 *  4.18 works too.
339:		 *
340:		 *  By extrapolating a bit (allowing for variations for other
341:		 *  versions of the PROM), let's return if the read of the
342:		 *  CRIME_REV register occurs anywhere near 0xbfc051XX.
343:		 */
344:		if (((uint32_t)cpu->pc & 0xffffff00) == (uint32_t)0xbfc05100) {
345:			cpu->pc = cpu->cd.mips.gpr[MIPS_GPR_RA];
346:			if (cpu->is_32bit)
347:				mips32_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
348:			else
349:				mips_pc_to_pointers(cpu);
350:		}
351:		break;
352:
353:	case CRIME_CONTROL:	/*  0x008  */
354:		/*  TODO: 64-bit write to CRIME_CONTROL, but some things
355:		    (such as NetBSD 1.6.2) write to 0x00c!  */
356:		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
357:			/*
358:			 *  0x200 = watchdog timer (according to NetBSD)
359:			 *  0x800 = "reboot" used by the IP32 PROM
360:			 */
361:			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE) {
362:				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_DOG_ENABLE;
363:			}
364:			if (idata & CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET) {
365:				int j;
366:
367:				/*  This is used by the IP32 PROM's
368:				    "reboot" command:  */
369:				for (j=0; j<cpu->machine->ncpus; j++)
370:					cpu->machine->cpus[j]->running = 0;
371:
372:				idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_HARD_RESET;
373:			}
374:
375:			idata &= ~CRIME_CONTROL_ENDIANESS;
376:
377:			if (idata != 0)
378:				fatal("[ CRIME_CONTROL: unimplemented "
379:				    "control 0x%016llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
380:		}
381:		break;
382:
383:	case CRIME_INTSTAT:	/*  0x010, Current interrupt status  */
384:	case CRIME_INTMASK:	/*  0x018, Current interrupt mask  */

82:     session: lb/lh/sb/sh said, hi said), and its CHANNEL -- fatal(), which prints when the guest free-runs, where a
83:     debug() would not -- on EVERY axis of the refusal: bits 0-2 (free said, +0x07), bits 3-8 and len 2 (free 2r said,
84:     +0x1f8), bit 9 and a write (free 1w said, +0x27f), a 2-byte write at a label (free 2w said, CONTROL), and each
85:     remaining label (free rev/istat/imask/dog/time said).  *** Until repair 10 the channel was pinned at ONE cell (+0x07):
86:     `(relative_addr & 8 ? debug : fatal)(' and `(len == 2 ? debug : fatal)(' each scored 131/131 on the 131-row test (codex, the Opus
87:     seat, fourth closing pass).  One refusal per free-running session -- the latch silences any second one. ***
88:  6. the ACCEPT SET shrunk at any single cell (the de-escalation clause: the deleted exit(1) used to announce this),
89:     at BOTH ends of the window:  the FIRST slot +0x00 -- acc8 near (8R, anchored to CRIME_REV's 0x11, which the
90:     prelude writes; grok, closing pass: `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0' passed the 85-row version, whose rows sat at
91:     +0x30 -- an interior slot, not an end), with devsig the 4R-low cell there and `rev pc ctl' the 4R-high one (its
92:     redirect needs the access ACCEPTED; `devsig hi' reads 0 either way -- grok, codex, second closing pass, correcting
93:     this sentence, which credited devsig hi); CRIME_DOG +0x30 -- acc8 st+ld
94:     (8W, 8R), acc4 rhi, acc4 rlo, acc4 wlo, acc4 whi; the last slot +0x278 -- top8 st+ld, top4 rhi, top4 rlo, top4 w.  Those rows are ROUND TRIPS and cannot see an
95:     accept arm that re-maps its register index (kimi-k3:cloud, F2: `d->reg[(relative_addr & 0x3f) / 8]' passed the
96:     71-row version), so one row is ANCHORED: `top8 anchor' reads bank-1 control at +0x210, a value the PRELUDE writes
97:     by bank index (1 for -M 64), not by the decode under test; `top4 anchor' reads its low half with a 4R.  The 4R
98:     of its HIGH half answers 0 accepted or refused, so that cell is pinned by the ANNOUNCE instead (Opus closing:
99:     `len == 4 && relative_addr != 0x210' passed the 85-row version): every accept cell the top session touches
100:     before its one refusal is pinned the same way                                                      top said
101:     *** It pins the model's CURRENT bank layout, which the
102:     filed `crimebankorder' disputes; a round that re-orders the banks owes this row's re-derivation. ***
103:  7. a refused store that LANDS: CRIME_DOG seeded by an accepted sd survives a refused sb and sh     sb keeps, sh keeps;
104:     and a seeded slot with address bit 4 CLEAR (+0x40; the Opus third-closing seat, measured: every read-back refused
105:     store had bit 4 set, so a landing keyed on bit 4 clear passed), including a refused HALFWORD with address bit 1
106:     SET at +0x42 (codex, the Opus seat, fourth closing pass: every read-back refused 2-byte store cleared bit 1, and a
107:     2W landing keyed on it scored 131/131 on the 131-row test)                                                             lo keeps
108:     and at the far block, a refused sb +0x27f and sh +0x27c leave the SEEDED +0x278 as seeded (Opus, F2 and closing: a
109:     store landing only >= 0x200, of either width; this said "reading 0" until the fourth closing pass -- repair 8
110:     seeded the slot)                                                                                   hi keeps
111:     and in the middle of the window (+0x1ff / +0x1fc, every address bit 0-8 set)                       mid keeps
112:     and at INTMASK, read back by an accepted ld (kimi CLI, second closing pass: a landing that ORs into INTMASK kept
113:     bit 28, so imask ctl could not see it)                                                             imask keeps
114:  8. state carried ACROSS calls: a declined read after accepted reads of a nonzero register still answers 0 (an
115:     `odata' made static), and an accepted access between two refusals does not re-arm the announce    warm v0,
116:     warm once  (the codex seat, F2) -- closed over every place an accepted access can go after codex found the
117:     4-byte arm's re-arm open on the closing pass -- now the KIND x LABEL CROSS, the SAME list as item 4's pre-set
118:     (`ACCEPTS', 43 kinds; codex, second closing pass: `d->warned = 0;' inside DOG_ENABLE; the Opus third-closing seat:
119:     CONTROL's read, DOG-clear and zero-leftover sides; the fourth closing pass: a re-arm on an accepted WRITE at
120:     INTSTAT/INTMASK or REV and on CONTROL's 0x0200 cell, each 131/131 on the 131-row test) -- each accepted kind between two refusals
121:                                                                                            rearm once, rearm surv
122:     and in the middle block (an accepted sd between two refusals there)                                mid once
123:  9. the ANSWER at each named label: a refused read at CRIME_REV, CONTROL, INTSTAT, INTMASK, DOG and TIME answers 0
124:     (codex, closing pass: `if (relative_addr == CRIME_REV) odata = 1;' passed the 85-row version -- the REV rows
125:     checked only the pc)          lb/lh rev v0, ctl v0, istat/imask lb/lh v0, dog v0, dog lh v0, time lb/lh v0
126:     every one at 2 bytes (lh sees every value lb sees) and over a register whose LOW BYTES ARE NONZERO, so a decline
127:     that answers the register shows (kimi CLI, second closing pass: INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG held 0 in their low
128:     bytes and `odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]' there passed; codex: `odata = 0x100' at DOG passed the lb-only row)
129:     -- REV 0x11, CONTROL 0x100, TIME 66-990 (low bytes); INTSTAT/INTMASK 0x0101010111010101 and DOG
130:     0x0123456789abcdef, nonzero in EVERY byte, so any SHIFT of the register's bytes into the answer shows (Opus, second
131:     closing pass: the ADDRESSED lanes passed the 0x10000001 seed 121/121 on the 121-row test; glm, third closing pass:
132:     the 4-byte path's half, `reg >> 32', passed the 0x0101000010000001 one).  [A selection CONFINED above byte 1 --
133:     `& 0xffff0000' -- answers 0 to every lb/lh by construction and is EQUIVALENT; this said "ANY selection ... shows"
134:     until the kimi CLI seat, fourth closing pass.]  And the answer SOURCE other than the addressed register: every
135:     lvalue the function reads is nonzero where a refused answer is read, including HARDINT -- latched only in the gate
136:     session, where refused lb/lh now read it (kimi-k3:cloud, the Opus seat, fourth closing pass: a decline answering
137:     `d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / 8]' scored 131/131 on the 131-row test)                                          gate lb v0, gate lh v0.
138:     CONTROL is answered at both widths (ctl lb v0, ctl v0; the Opus
139:     third-closing seat, measured: `odata = 1' keyed on len 1 at CONTROL passed the lh-only row); its low byte is 0, so
140:     only a CONSTANT answer can show at lb there.  TIME: at the time rows' refused reads TIME is a multiple of 66 and is
141:     0 in 42 of 800 logged cells (the same seat -- this said "0.3% of runs, 66-990 includes 256, 512, 768", and those
142:     are not multiples of 66), so the register cell is pinned by a SEEDED odd TIME instead     time sd lb v0, time sd lh v0
143:     and in the far block and the middle block over a NONZERO slot, at both widths (Opus closing: a refused read
144:     >= 0x200 answering the register; Opus, second closing pass: the 2-byte cell there)
145:                                                                   top rd v0, top rd2 v0, mid lb v0, mid lh v0
146:  10. the decline HOLDS WHILE AN INTERRUPT IS LATCHED: HARDINT set by the guest through MACE and the CRIME line
147:      asserted, a refused sh 0x0800 at CONTROL still does not stop the CPUs (the Opus closing seat: `if
148:      (!d->reg[CRIME_HARDINT / 8])' -- a field the tail reads -- and `if (!d->prev_asserted)' passed the 85-row
149:      version)                                        gate run, with gate hardint and gate line as its controls
150:      and a refused READ answers 0 while HARDINT is latched -- the answer SOURCE item 9 names (fourth closing pass)
151:                                                                                                 gate lb v0, gate lh v0
152:
153:DISCLOSED, not rowed:
154:  * `goto crime_declined' -> `return 1' is EQUIVALENT on every channel: memory_rw pre-zeroes a device read buffer
155:    (#95, src/cpus/memory_rw.c -- `if (writeflag == MEM_READ)' then `memset(data, 0, len);', immediately above
156:    `paddr -= mem->devices[i].baseaddr;'), and on a declined access nothing between the entry and the tail writes
157:    HARDINT or SOFTINT, so the tail's restore and recompute are identities.  Measured 40/40 (the then 40-row test) in kill table 1.
158:  * a fall-through confined to the two label cells whose arms have NO body for that access, both EQUIVALENT:
159:      CRIME_DOG (0x30)       `No warning.' -- the arm is `break;'
160:      CRIME_CONTROL, reads   the arm acts only on MEM_WRITE
161:    Each measured as a survivor in kill table 2 (`!= CRIME_DOG', the CONTROL-read-only fall-through).
162:  * ACCEPT cells at unrowed slots: the rows touch accepted accesses at +0x00, +0x04, +0x08, +0x10, +0x18, +0x30,
163:    +0x34, +0x38, +0x210, +0x214, +0x1f8 and +0x278 (not an exhaustive list of what the window accepts); an accept arm
164:    narrowed by an address strictly INSIDE the window at an unrowed slot (e.g. `len == 8 && relative_addr != 0x100',
165:    kill table 2's acc8mid, 71/71 on the 71-row test) is not seen.
166:  * an effect ADDED to the decline arm and keyed on ONE UNNAMED offset (item 9 rows the READ answer at every named
167:    label) -- a refused store that lands only at
168:    +0x31, a declined read answering nonzero only at +0x20, a silent decline only at +0x100, a fall-through only at
169:    +0x40 (kimi CLI, glm, F2).  The rows pin the refusal floor at every observable LABEL and at eight default-arm
170:    offsets; the keeps rows catch a landing at CRIME_DOG, INTMASK, the far block and the middle block.  An effect
171:    keyed on a single ADDRESS BIT is NOT in this class: every bit 0-9 is carried both ways by a refused access of each
172:    kind that can carry it (the mid session sets bits 0-8, the far block bit 9; an aligned 2-byte access never sets
173:    bit 0; kimi-k3:cloud, second closing pass, found bits 7 and 8 carried by no refusal at all; codex and the Opus seat,
174:    fourth closing pass, found 2W LANDING at bit 1 set carried by no read-back store -- now +0x42, `lo keeps').  Note
175:    `relative_addr / 8': a refused halfword at +0x1e would land on INTMASK's slot, not at +0x1e; the 2W session's
176:    +0x1e store is not read back, and the INTMASK landing is carried by `imask keeps'.  A landing at
177:    CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20, not a switch label) is in this class (deepseek, third closing pass).  ONE NAMED CELL IS
178:    OPEN AND SAID SO (kimi-k3:cloud, closing pass): a refused sb/sh that LANDS only at CRIME_TIME stores <= 0xffff,
179:    which the time rows' 0.1 s threshold cannot tell from the 66-990 the first tick adds -- clause (e) debt.  At INTSTAT
180:    (the tail recomputes it), CONTROL and REV (the prelude rewrites them) such a landing is EQUIVALENT.
181:  * an accepted WRITE (4 or 8 bytes) at +0x00 or +0x08 shrunk away: the prelude rewrites CRIME_REV and CRIME_CONTROL on
182:    every access, so no round trip there can observe whether the store was accepted (the CONTROL arm's effects are
183:    rowed separately as sw08 halts / sw08 msg ctl).  The 4R-high cell at +0x00 is carried by `rev pc ctl' (its redirect
184:    needs the access ACCEPTED), not by `devsig hi', whose 0 a refused read also returns.
185:  * a decline answering a selection of the register's bytes at CRIME_REV, CRIME_CONTROL or CRIME_TIME that the model
186:    holds at 0 there -- the ADDRESSED lanes (bytes 0-1 at a label), or the 4-byte half above the low word -- is
187:    EQUIVALENT (the prelude rewrites REV = 0x11 and CONTROL = 0x100 on every access; TIME is kept to 32 bits).  At
188:    INTSTAT, INTMASK and DOG every byte is seeded nonzero, so any selection shows (Opus, second closing pass; glm, third).
189:  * a latch keyed on a JOINT predicate (e.g. only 2-byte refusals at CRIME_TIME), or on one unnamed offset other than the
190:    default-arm cells the latch rows refuse at: clause (e), the single-axis generating set being the floor.
191:  * a re-arm or pre-set inside the HARD_RESET branch of the CONTROL arm: an accepted write that takes it stops every
192:    CPU, so nothing can follow it in the session.  Unrowable, not merely unrowed.
193:  * a fall-through only at CRIME_SOFTINT (0x20), which the switch does not name -- measured 71/71 (the 71-row test) by the Opus seat
194:    (F2, `sft'); it is the single-unnamed-offset class of the bullet above.
195:  * a FUNCTION-STATIC latch in place of `d->warned' is NOT equivalent in general -- two IP32 machines in one gxemul
196:    process would share it -- and this single-machine probe cannot see it (kimi-k3:cloud, F2; the pass-2 Opus seat had
197:    recorded it "equivalent", which holds only for one machine per process).
198:  * widths 3, 5, 6, 7 (no MIPS load/store issues them); the unaligned-pair instructions (lwl/lwr/swl/swr, ldl/ldr) are
199:    not rowed.  The RAM-size checks ABOVE the width arm (`-M 48' exits at the first CRIME access) are filed, not here.
200:  * independence between two CRIME instances (the latch is per instance by construction; IP32 has one CRIME).
201:
202:Sentinel: CRIMEP_DONE (plus CRIMEP_RESULT=n/N and CRIMEP_CONTROL=OK|FAIL).
203:"""
204:import os, pty, re, select, signal, sys, time
205:
206:BIN = sys.argv[1]
207:LABEL = sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv) > 2 else "?"
208:DUMMY = "/tmp/dummy_crimep.bin"
209:if not os.path.exists(DUMMY):
210:    with open(DUMMY, "wb") as f:
211:        f.write(b"\0" * 16)
212:
213:CODE = 0x80001000
214:SCRATCH_RAM = 0xa0001800
215:LIVE_VAL = 0x12345678
216:CRIME = 0xb4000000
217:SENT = 0xdeadbeef
218:RA = 0x80004000
219:PROBE_PC = 0xbfc05180           # inside the CRIME_REV hack's window, (pc & 0xffffff00) == 0xbfc05100
220:
221:R = {"zero": 0, "at": 1, "v0": 2, "v1": 3, "a0": 4, "a1": 5, "a2": 6, "a3": 7, "t0": 8, "t1": 9,
222:     "s0": 16, "s1": 17, "s2": 18, "s3": 19, "ra": 31}
223:OPC = {"lb": 0x20, "lh": 0x21, "lw": 0x23, "lbu": 0x24, "lhu": 0x25, "ld": 0x37,
224:       "sb": 0x28, "sh": 0x29, "sw": 0x2b, "sd": 0x3f}
225:OP_B_SELF = 0x1000ffff
226:OP_NOP = 0
227:
228:
229:def enc(op, rs, rt, imm=0):
230:    return (OPC[op] << 26) | (R[rs] << 21) | (R[rt] << 16) | (imm & 0xffff)
231:
232:
233:#  hand-assembled encodings checked against the committed witness's constants (a wrong register field reads 0, which
234:#  a decline's own answer would accept by accident -- so the accept rows carry nonzero wants)
235:assert enc("lb", "at", "v0") == 0x80220000 and enc("lhu", "at", "v0") == 0x94220000
236:assert enc("sb", "a3", "t0") == 0xa0e80000 and enc("sh", "a3", "t0") == 0xa4e80000
237:assert enc("sw", "a3", "t0") == 0xace80000 and enc("lw", "at", "v0") == 0x8c220000
238:
239:rows = []
240:
241:
242:def row(name, got, want, note=""):
243:    ok = (got == want)
244:    rows.append((name, ok))
245:    print("%-16s %-20s %-20s %-40s %s" % (name, str(got), str(want), note, "ok" if ok else "FAIL"))
246:    sys.stdout.flush()
247:
248:
249:def session(cmds, settle=1.5, spin=1.2):
250:    """one emulator, read to the pty's EOF after `quit'.  Returns (text, clean): clean = EOF reached AND exit 0."""
251:    pid, fd = pty.fork()
252:    if pid == 0:
253:        os.execv(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-x", "-E", "sgi", "-e", "ip32", "-M", "64", "0x80001000:" + DUMMY])
254:        os._exit(127)
255:    buf = ""
256:
257:    def rd(t):
258:        nonlocal buf
259:        end = time.time() + t
260:        while time.time() < end:
261:            r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], 0.1)
262:            if not r:
263:                continue
264:            try:
265:                d = os.read(fd, 65536)
266:            except OSError:
267:                return False
268:            if not d:
269:                return False
270:            buf += d.decode("latin-1", "replace")
271:        return True
272:
273:    rd(settle)
274:    for i, c in enumerate(cmds):
275:        try:
276:            if c == "<RUN>":        # free-run: `continue', let the guest run, then break back in with CTRL-C
277:                os.write(fd, b"continue\n")
278:                ok = rd(2.0)
279:                os.write(fd, b"\x03")
280:                ok = ok and rd(1.5)
281:            else:
282:                os.write(fd, (c + "\n").encode("latin-1"))
283:                ok = rd(spin if i == len(cmds) - 1 else 0.25)
284:        except OSError:
285:            break
286:        if not ok:
287:            break
288:    eof = True                      # a process already gone before `quit' has closed the pty; its status says how
289:    try:
290:        os.write(fd, b"quit\n")
291:        eof = not rd(10.0)          # to EOF, so anything printed on the way out is in the transcript
292:    except OSError:
293:        pass
294:    if not eof:
295:        try:
296:            os.kill(pid, signal.SIGKILL)    # a session that never reached EOF is ended, never waited on forever
297:        except OSError:
298:            pass
299:    try:
300:        os.close(fd)
301:    except OSError:
302:        pass
303:    how = "nostatus"
304:    try:
305:        _, stat = os.waitpid(pid, 0)
306:        how = ("sig%d" % os.WTERMSIG(stat)) if os.WIFSIGNALED(stat) else ("exit%d" % os.WEXITSTATUS(stat))
307:    except OSError:
308:        pass
309:    return buf.replace("\r", ""), (eof and how == "exit0")
310:
311:
312:def pr(t, reg):
313:    m = re.search(r"print\s+%s\s*\n\s*(0x[0-9a-f]+)" % reg, t)
314:    return ("0x%x" % int(m.group(1), 16)) if m else "absent"
315:
316:
317:def exc(t):
318:    c = pr(t, "cause")
319:    return ("%d" % ((int(c, 16) >> 2) & 0x1f)) if c != "absent" else "absent"
320:
321:
322:def count(t, s, clean):
323:    """a text count is evidence only from a whole transcript: an unclean session reads `unclean', never a number"""
325:
326:
327:class Addr(object):
328:    def __init__(self, base):
329:        self.a = base
330:
331:    def take(self, n):
332:        got = self.a
333:        self.a += 4 * (n + 2) + 0x20
334:        return got
335:
336:
337:def run_ops(ops, at):
338:    put = ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * i, op) for i, op in enumerate(ops)]
339:    put += ["put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops), OP_B_SELF),
340:            "put w 0x%x, 0x%08x" % (at + 4 * len(ops) + 4, OP_NOP)]
341:    return put + ["pc=0x%x" % at, "step %d" % len(ops)]
342:
343:
344:def ld(A, op, addr, rt, at=None):
345:    """one load `op rt, 0(at)', rt seeded with SENT; returns (cmds, code address)"""
346:    here = A.take(1) if at is None else at
347:    return ["at=0x%x" % addr, "%s=0x%x" % (rt, SENT)] + run_ops([enc(op, "at", rt)], here), here
348:
349:
350:def st(A, op, addr, val, at=None):
351:    here = A.take(1) if at is None else at
352:    return ["a3=0x%x" % addr, "t0=0x%x" % val] + run_ops([enc(op, "a3", "t0")], here), here
353:
354:
355:AFTER = ["print pc", "print cause"]
356:
357:
358:def roundtrip(A):
359:    """a RAM round trip printed in v1: absent if the host died, the sentinel if the CPU was stopped"""
360:    c1, _ = st(A, "sw", SCRATCH_RAM, LIVE_VAL)
361:    c2, _ = ld(A, "lw", SCRATCH_RAM, "v1")
362:    return c1 + c2 + ["print v1"]
363:
364:
365:def said(t, ln, off, clean):
366:    """the whole announce, `(once)' marker included -- a reworded or unmarked announce reads 0"""
367:    return count(t, "unimplemented len=%d at 0x%x -- declined.  (once) ]" % (ln, off), clean)
368:
369:
370:def dflt(t, clean, off, write):
371:    """the register switch's default-arm debug() line FOR THIS REFUSED ACCESS -- `[ crime: read from 0xOFF,' or
372:    `[ crime: write to 0xOFF:' -- printed while stepping, so a refused access that falls into the switch at an
373:    unlabelled offset says so here.  Keyed on the access's own offset and direction, because an ACCEPTED access at a
374:    default-arm offset in the same session prints the same kind of line (hi keeps' ld +0x278 does)."""
375:    if not clean:
376:        return "unclean"
377:    return t.count(("[ crime: write to 0x%x:" if write else "[ crime: read from 0x%x,") % off)

513:# ---- the INTSTAT / INTMASK labels: their arm calls crime_interrupt_reassert(), which is NOT idempotent -- ----
514:#  INTMASK = bit 28 and SOFTINT = bit 28 are stored with 8-byte writes; SOFTINT's store goes to the default arm and does
515:#  NOT reassert, so the CPU's IP2 (cause bit 0x400) stays low until something reasserts.  A refused access at the label
516:#  must leave it low; the accepted `lw' that follows is the CONTROL that the arm reasserts at once.  (Measured by the
517:#  pass-2 Opus seat, opus_pass2_20260913/scripts/crime_p2_escape.py E1/E2: a fall-through at the label sets IP2.)
518:#  BIT carries bit 0 as well (the kimi CLI seat, second closing pass): a refused read answers the low bytes of whatever
519:#  the decline hands the tail, so with a register whose low bytes were 0 a decline that answered THE REGISTER
520:#  (`odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8]' at INTSTAT or INTMASK) read back 0 and passed.  AND in its TOP lanes (the Opus
521:#  second-closing seat, measured 121/121 on the 0x10000001 seed): a decline that answers the bytes the ADDRESS names --
522:#  `odata = d->reg[relative_addr / 8] >> (8 * (8 - len - (relative_addr & 7)))', the lane the model's own 4-byte read
523:#  path uses -- reads bytes 0-1 at the label, which the 0x10000001 seed left 0.  So the seed is nonzero in byte 0, byte 1
524:#  and the low byte; INTSTAT = SOFTINT | HARDINT and INTMASK both carry it; the reassert still sees bit 28.
525:#  And glm (third closing pass) found a THIRD byte selection -- the 4-byte path's half, `reg >> 32' -- whose low bytes
526:#  that seed left 0.  So the seed is now nonzero in EVERY byte: no selection of the register's bytes can answer 0.  (DOG,
527:#  the far slot and the middle slot are seeded every-byte-nonzero already.)
528:BIT = 0x0101010111010101

609:# ---- the TIME label: an accepted read returns the value of the PREVIOUS update, then updates -- so a refused access
610:#  that reached crime_update_crime_time() would show in the NEXT read.  The first `ld' latches last_microseconds; on the
611:#  fixed build the only other update before the next `ld' is the device tick's FIRST firing -- ticks_till_next starts at
612:#  0, so it fires right after the session's first `ld' (the Opus closing seat; this said "nothing else updates TIME"
613:#  until then), and it adds the few microseconds since the latch: the 66-990 the rows print (measured across 52 cells
614:#  against SMALL = 6.6e6).  Later ticks are 2^14 cycles apart and a session steps a few dozen instructions; a fall-through at 0x38
615:#  makes it the wall-clock interval of the intervening debugger commands, ~1e8.  The third `ld' is the CONTROL that the
616:#  channel moves.  (Opus pass 2, E3; the tick-spacing wording corrected by the kimi CLI seat on the F2 pass.)
617:#  ASSUMPTION, disclosed: after that FIRST tick, no further tick falls between the first `ld' and the `ld' that samples

620:#  dev_crime_tick also calls crime_interrupt_reassert(), so a tick inside a session could raise IP2 on the fixed build
